#2670. Aspect analysis: Leipzig never found a trigger – LC

von Next Generation am 20.10.2026 in den Kategorien Uncategorized mit 0 Kommentaren

A 6-1 reads like a game in which everything fell apart. Look at how the goals came about one by one and a different picture emerges: it was not six separate problems but two or three, which Bayern punished in seven variations. No pressure on the man in possession, marking problems in central areas, and a back line that did not work with the movements in front of it – that chain runs through practically every goal conceded.

This aspect analysis therefore takes the goals rather than the game as its material and works back from there to the patterns.

Bayern triggered very high throughout, stayed tight to their opponents and pushed through centrally without hesitation. Vandevoordt was pressed from Bayern’s right, with the clear intention of keeping him away from the left-hand build-up route and forcing the long ball. Leipzig’s front line of Nusa and Brajan Gruda repeatedly angled their runs deep against Nathaniel Brown and Konrad Laimer.

Bayern had one extra central player dropping into midfield in this phase, usually Kimmich. Had Leipzig played short to the right, he could have pressed through onto Raum; in practice he stayed central and pushed through from there. That left Leipzig with the long ball, and it was ineffective: it produced almost no regains, so the enforced forward play brought neither relief nor territory.

Going the other way, Leipzig got no access at all, and the reason was Bayern’s rotations. Kimmich dropped right, Pavlović left into a back three, with Upamecano holding a very wide position. Against this shifting three-man build-up Leipzig never found a trigger, because the assignments reset with every rotation – whoever was supposed to step out of midfield first had to work out who he was picking up. The handover of pressing duties out of central areas failed repeatedly.

In front of that, Bayern staggered their shape so that Leipzig’s compactness became the problem. Olise moved into the ten space at times, Nathaniel Brown operated in the half-lane and ran beyond from there, Luis Díaz stayed wide left. Saibari and Kane pinned several opponents centrally while regularly pulling out of the central lane. Because Leipzig pressed very narrow, this produced exactly what Bayern were looking for: once the opponent was committed near the ball, the switch followed, and on the now open side stood a one-on-one.

That already builds the bridge to the goals. The isolated one-on-ones in which Luis Díaz got his three finishes were not individual moments but the product of this sequence of pinning and switching.

1-0, Luis Díaz (12.) – The first goal already contains the full diagnosis. Kimmich receives centrally without any pressure and can turn out with an open body shape. Ahead of him Saibari and Kane occupy the central zone in front of the box, which forces Orban and Lukeba to step out – so far, the intended part. The full-backs, however, do not push up with that movement and stay too deep, while Raum’s position plays the runner onside. Klostermann stays outside and leaves the inside lane unsecured, and that is exactly where Luis Díaz starts from. Out of that unprotected lane Díaz runs into the back line and Kimmich chips the ball in behind. Not one bad decision, but four players whose decisions did not fit together.

2-0, Aleksandar Pavlović (26.) – Same cause, different route. Kane drops deep and Leipzig do not shift across as a unit – the reactions showed it clearly: arms thrown up, gesturing, hands dropping again, the beginnings of frustration. Kane gets the free body shape, and with him the consequence is predictable: the long ball, high or flat. Because Leipzig are so narrow centrally, the ball flies straight through the entire zone in front of their back line. Luis Díaz moves actively into the passing lane to control it while Klostermann tries to get into his running line and does not make it – Díaz reaches the ball first. From the one-on-one he wins, Díaz dribbles centrally at Orban, which creates a four-against-three on the break. Díaz pins Orban and Lukeba centrally, Pavlović overlaps on the left after holding a high, left-leaning position all evening and threatening the back line. Two touches, left foot, finished away from the defender.

3-0, Michael Olise (34.) – Here Bayern’s press shows itself at its clearest. From a Leipzig throw-in, the man-orientation is fully visible: consistent pushing through, clean handovers. A misplaced pass from Raum then cannot be defended, because neither the tracking nor the duelling is good enough to disturb the pass. Kane can open up half-way. Centrally, Olise comes inside from the wing, and Banzuzi and his partner fail to pick him up, letting him run through – against a player of that variability, an invitation to the key pass. Luis Díaz runs beyond and drags Orban with him, Klostermann is pinned wide by Saibari and can no longer get back into central areas. Olise finishes against the goalkeeper’s movement, across his standing leg into the far corner, catching Vandevoordt completely on the wrong foot.

4-0, Luis Díaz (47.) – After a regain, Bayern switch quickly to the far side and once diagonally through, putting Díaz in a one-on-one again. He holds the ball in the dribble, and Leipzig fail to solve the situation in three steps: the centre is not closed, Orban is too late stepping out, Klostermann does not get into the duel. That Vandevoordt leaves the far corner open is the final piece of an isolated one-on-one that should never have arisen.

5-0, Harry Kane (53.) – Structurally the most interesting goal. Leipzig play a long ball without preparing it and react to the consequences contradictorily: the back line does not push up at all, while the midfield line and both forwards counterpress forwards. The ten space opens up enormously, the gaps between the lines become huge – and Olise is standing in that zone. One pass from Kim is enough to take six Leipzig players out of the game. Olise runs unopposed at the back line and lays it off to Díaz, who is in a one-on-one again; Brown joins for the two-against-one and shifts into the inside lane. Klostermann does not pick up his run from the deep half-space, and centrally Kane again drops into Orban’s blind side – a pattern that stood out across the whole game. The result is a two-against-one against the goalkeeper.

6-0, Luis Díaz (69.) – From a set piece Bayern create a five-against-four out wide. Gnabry pins opponents and makes the space for Díaz, Orban again takes one big step in the block and does not get into the duel. The same scene as the 4-0, only from a different phase of play – a sign that this is a behavioural pattern rather than a snapshot.

6-1, Andrija Maksimović (90.+1) – Leipzig’s goal shows in reverse what their own game had been missing. Bayern drop into a deeper block here, and Leipzig combine centrally before going wide. Gruda gets into a one-on-one against Brown, Upamecano steps across – but from above, with too wide a gap and without closing the centre. That opens the one-two, which Leipzig take immediately. Because the whole situation narrows onto that combination, the space behind it stays unoccupied, and Maksimović can take two touches on his left and finish unopposed.

The sequence of goals reads like a repetition. Five of Bayern’s six come from the same combination of no pressure on the ball centrally and a back line that does not manage the space behind it. Luis Díaz stands in an isolated one-on-one three times, Orban is twice too late with a step that is too big, Kane repeatedly drops into his blind side. Klostermann certainly did not have his best day – but it would be too easy to settle the bill with him. In the decisive scenes he faced situations that functioning protection in front of him should never have allowed to arise in the first place.

Behind it all lies not an individual problem but a structural one: Leipzig pressed narrow and found no trigger against Bayern’s rotating back three. That very compactness made the switch of play the opponent’s most effective tool, and at the end of every switch stood a one-on-one that Leipzig lost. The scoreline is not the story – the story is that the same scene worked six times.

LC is often found in a classroom, happiest on a football pitch, and occasionally on LinkedIn.

Simone Inzaghi rivede nella Lazio di Gattuso alcune caratteristiche della squadra che aveva allenato e torna sul rimpianto per lo scudetto sfumato dopo lo stop causato dalla pandemia. CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il rimpianto che dice di portare ancora con sé riguarda ciò che sarebbe potuto accadere senza il Covid.

Alla vigilia di Venezia-Inter, Davide Nicola è intervenuto in conferenza stampa per presentare la gara del Penzo. "Sfide complicate come lo sono tutte e come lo saranno tutte da qui alla fine, chi per un motivo e chi per un altro. Io parlo di sfide che ci danno la possibilità di crescere ulteriormente. Dal mio arrivo abbiamo fatto quasi due settimane di lavoro, con tre partite di mezzo, comprendendo quella di domani. Ho potuto valutare le caratteristiche dei giocatori in maniera più specifica, stiamo recuperando giocatori come Busio e Bella-Kotchap che sono stati integrati per recuperare ritmo partita, vediamo ora le possibilità di minutaggio che potranno avere per farli crescere".

"C'è sempre l'ambizione di essere competitivi e di fare punti, sappiamo che non è facile contro certe realtà. Ma se devo analizzare un processo logico di pensiero penso che a Genova abbiamo avuto i numeri per meritare di vincerla, nella partita dove abbiamo concesso di meno in assoluto. Eppure l'abbiamo persa: questa è la Serie A, l'ho detto ai ragazzi. Dobbiamo essere coscienti del fatto che non basta fare le cose bene, bisogna farle meglio degli altri".

"Di contro non ci si aspettava forse la vittoria contro il Napoli. Due partite, tre punti, bisogna sempre avere l'attenzione al risultato, ma in questo momento è anche sul processo di crescita e credo che l'Inter arrivi a fare il sunto di tutto ciò che serve ad una squadra come la nostra per essere competitivi a certi livelli. Ci siamo preparati con attenzione, abbiamo recuperato, domani vedremo".

Despite being named among the substitutes in each of Milan’s opening three Serie A matches, Pulisic has watched every minute from the bench as the new coach searches for the right balance in attack.

#2665. Michael Carrick future: Man United boss addresses "tough" situation as next manager likely to be appointed in coming days

By Lewis Nolan | 25 Oct 2026 20:06

Michael Carrick has admitted that that he does not know what the future holds for him at Manchester United following yet another defeat.

Despite playing in front of a home crowd, the Red Devils suffered a 3-0 defeat in the Premier League against Bournemouth on Sunday.

That result left the club in 16th place, and they now only have seven points from a possible 24, while they are just one point above the relegation zone.

Head coach Carrick is under significant pressure, and speaking to Sky Sports, he admitted he does not know if he will still be in the dugout next weekend, saying: "It's a tough situation. There's no hiding from it. What it looks like, what's next, I really don't know.

"I always do what's best for this football club. I've said it all along and I don't change that now. "I love what I do. I love the position I'm in. I feel the responsibility."

United are set to play Chelsea on Saturday, though it would be surprising if he was still in charge on Monday.

"It wasn't a nice way to end the game" Michael Carrick reacts after Manchester United's 3-0 defeat to Bournemouth ?️ https://t.co/Qxaz7GxQSL

A number of managers have been linked to Old Trafford, including Brighton & Hove Albion boss Fabian Hurzeler, though he has reportedly distanced himself from the role.

Other leading candidates are said to include Eddie Howe and Antonio Conte, both of whom have extensive Premier League experience.

United need as much stability as possible, so perhaps the infamous volatility of Conte would make him a poor appointment.

However, considering the Red Devils have had three permanent managers since October 2024, it remains to be seen if Carrick's successor will be given the time they need.

United's summer transfer window was alarming, as while they focused on strengthening in the middle of the pitch, they neglected other areas of need.

The club's frontline are rarely productive, with there serious concerns about the form of Matheus Cunha and the fitness of Benjamin Sesko.

At the back, defenders like Harry Maguire, Luke Shaw, Diogo and Lisandro Martinez have struggled for some time, and there is an argument that all four need to be replaced.

Younger defenders like Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven have struggled to adapt to a toxic environment at Old Trafford, and while Carrick has disappointed, he may ultimately be on the verge of being sacked because he was failed in the transfer market.

#2664. Access, Not Chances: How Como Took Manchester United Apart – LC

von Next Generation am 24.10.2026 in den Kategorien Champions League,Uncategorized mit 0 Kommentaren

Como beat Manchester United 4-1 — and in this case the scoreline really is a fair expression of the game. For long stretches the match was one-sided, not in terms of chance volume but in terms of access: Como dictated in every phase where the ball went, who received it, and under what conditions it had to be played on. United never got on terms in any of those phases.

Cesc Fàbregas’s side showed two things that rarely go together. Out of possession, a pressing structure with clear direction, isolation of one side, and man-marking that applied near the ball only for the action in progress while everything around it was already speculating on the second ball. And in possession, a positional game of remarkable technical maturity that ties opponents in, switches play, and works systematically into the space behind them. The 4-1 is therefore less an outlier than evidence of work that has been built up over recent seasons.

This analysis goes through the phases one by one: first Como in possession and United’s response, then the work against the ball from the high press to defending the box, and finally the goals.

In build-up, Como moved away from their nominal 4-2-3-1 and sat closer to a 4-3-3, with the eights as the real variable: very free in their movement and above all active in the half-spaces. The base structure stayed stable — a flat four plus a six in front of them, sometimes ahead of the line, sometimes slightly behind the forward line.

Close to their own goal, Como consistently involved goalkeeper Butez as an additional central player in the build-up. That created a five-against-four against United’s front line — awkward on paper, more awkward still in execution, because Como didn’t use the overload to play through but to tie opponents in. From there, the same shape kept appearing on both the right and the left: centre-back, full-back, a player from the half-position in the eight space, and a six alongside. A diamond that wasn’t an end in itself but had a purpose: the diagonal route out.

Como’s build-up structure. From a 4-1 (flat four), the half-positions with Paz and Perrone are added. Small triangles and diamonds emerge. The three attackers ahead of them create additional options in behind.

One detail deserves particular attention. At times the full-backs stood not wide but flat, in a half-position. That sounds like a nuance, but it had a clear function: it vacated the wing and created the space the eights could drop into — Paz and Perrone did so regularly. If you don’t occupy the wing yourself, you force the opponent to guard it anyway.

ManU got no access near the ball and left large diagonal spaces open on the far side.

For United’s midfielders this was close to unsolvable. Tielemans, Mount and Andrey Santos spent the whole game struggling to establish any responsibility at all, let alone get into duels. When an eight dropped out, they couldn’t follow, and if one of them was tied in by it, a two-against-one opened up on the far wing. Como didn’t create that situation once; they created it serially.

The decisive mechanism lay in receiving and relaying the ball. The player in the deeper position usually worked with two touches: the first took the ball backwards, the second relayed it to the free teammate. At the end of every one of those actions stood a player with the game in front of him — and from that open position he could switch play.

What made it possible were the preparatory movements. Paz and captain Da Cunha first lured their markers in the wrong direction and then worked with the head start they’d bought. That head start isn’t only space, it’s above all time: time to control the ball first and relay it second, rather than having to do both in one touch.

A scene in the 17th minute is typical. Mason Mount steps out, Mazraoui goes into the duel at the same time — nominally United are in an overload here. Only neither of them really gets to the opponent, because both are too late. Paz can therefore calmly take his touch backwards and switch play directly with the second, dissolving the pressure before it had even formed.

When the build-up ran down the right, it showed in a recurring pattern: Paz and Da Cunha took the first touch backwards or inside and then switched with the left foot out of a closed body position. The players they found were systematically in open positions — Milla, the far-side six, or the wing-to-wing connection directly.

In the second half, Como adjusted the shape and more often played with two sixes, to form the diamonds faster and switch to the far-side six faster. Couto stood out in this: the right-back repeatedly played the chip into the six or ten space — six or seven times in a row during one spell. It was mainly Paz who received the ball there and could launch the attack.

In the second half Como focus on those diagonal spaces and look for them mainly with chipped balls.

Especially striking inside the diamond are the rotations between the players. Because of the connections between them, they moved in rotation depending on running path, space and ball movement. The same recurring spaces were re-occupied. Under pressure on the ball carrier, the outer players pushed flat or ran in behind the opponents; the highest player in the diamond burst out of the cover shadow and became the reference point in depth. On a deep pass, the two wide players released and joined the depth, able to offer themselves again immediately in an open body position. If the pass went via the flat wide players, the spaces in front of the new ball carrier could be run into. Every rotation, every positional adjustment was coordinated with the others and, in the end, produced a stream of pressure-resolving passes while Manchester United chased the press in vain.

Como bridged the middle third exceptionally quickly, via dribbles at speed and diagonal passes. Behind it stood a recognisable principle: first tie the opponent to one wing, then get behind them via the diagonal, and in the final third do the same thing again. In sum, that produced the shape of a Z — build-up on the right, transition on the left, finish from the right again.

In this example Como pin ManU in the press with their short passing. Thanks to the extra attacker on the ball-near side they can play through the pressure and switch.

The connections between wingers and striker carried these attacks and were deliberately kept flexible. The wide players sometimes stayed wide, sometimes came short, sometimes tucked inside. Douvikas accordingly either waited deep in front of the line for the chipped ball or, when space opened up on the wing, received the deeper pass and then ran in behind along the wing as soon as the ball sat on the full-back’s open foot. That flexibility produced the overloads on one side, and the overloads produced the central overload.

In the final third one element was added that is rare in fast transition teams: patience. Como drew opponents towards them with dribbles and short passes, used back-passes when needed, and then switched in front of the block. Da Cunha, Baturina and Paz were the three who could tie opponents in and then find the diagonal. They created the positions on the diagonal, were available to receive, and used their space to apply pressure. The block was usually tied in with short touches, the switch and the position behind the opponent prepared, and quick finishes sought.

The intensity in possession shouldn’t be underestimated. Practically every attack involved at least three or four players, the width was occupied, the centre likewise, and there were multiple options throughout. What they looked for almost every time was the space behind the opponent on the far side — exactly the zone that is closed last when a block shifts across.

Paz brought the right profile for this pattern. A strong left foot cutting inside from the right, he repeatedly dropped into the right-hand blind side of his opponents, got shots away there with few touches, and racked up the most shots of the match. That the finishes generally came off few touches isn’t a detail but the consequence of the time won further back.

As the move continues, Como again look for the blind side of the defenders. Here Douvikas and Paz are positioned in behind, with Da Cunha joining in front of the back line. After briefly pinning the opponents, the switch reaches Da Cunha, who misses the target from distance.

What carries all of this is the technical execution. Soft passes, sharp ones when the moment called for it, consistently well timed and played into the right foot — for ninety minutes. Passing that secure at that tempo is what makes a variable concept actually retrievable.

United’s basic problem out of possession was the hybrid. They pressed half man-oriented, but with a plus-one in the last line — a construction that forces compromises in both directions. It demands access up front that it doesn’t fund at the back.

There were certainly moments when it worked. When Dalot pushed through into the press on the right and passed his man backwards, a clean man-orientation emerged all the way up to the front — and with it a clear assignment for Mount, out of which the ball could be won. But that stayed the exception. Most of the time Dalot sat too flat out of possession, so United couldn’t trigger the duels up front and went into the press a man short.

Mount was the most conspicuous case in this respect. He barely got into a duel across the whole match because the decision to step out always came too late. Against a team that works with preparatory movements, a late decision is practically the same as none: the opponent already has the head start before the journey even begins.

On top of that came a problem on the far side. There, United’s winger frequently failed to protect the depth — usually Mbeumo, who jogged back rather than closing the space. But that was precisely the target zone of Como’s diagonal switches. It allowed them to change sides almost at will and regularly find an open structure on the other side.

The second half brought no improvement. The distances to opponents stayed large, and centrally what was actually intended still didn’t materialise. Michael Carrick’s double change at half-time was meant to bring more physicality to the six with Carlos Baleba, but it didn’t solve the problem — the assignments still didn’t work, the duels in the centre still didn’t happen, and the wide positions dragged the eights out further, which opened new spaces.

In the end the energy simply wasn’t there to press with the necessary intensity. United’s press became increasingly static, and a static press against an opponent with this passing quality yields not a single ball win — it only hands them the starting positions they’re looking for anyway.

Como started the game in a high press and sustained it above all in the first half, with isolated spells after the break too. It was triggered on back-passes and goal kicks — and, remarkably, by their own goals: after every strike the team immediately pushed high again instead of managing the lead in a deeper block.

The steering ran consistently towards United’s left build-up side. The reason is personnel: Harry Maguire played there with his weaker left foot as the outside foot, and next to him stood another right-footer in Noussair Mazraoui on the left. Two right-footers on one build-up side means the ball always has to be taken back inside to play on — exactly where Como were closing the door. The press was usually triggered by the left-sided forward, with the ball ending up at either Maguire or Mazraoui.

Pressing structure after the change in the second half.

In the centre Como defended man-oriented, with both sixes pushing very high and taking flat passes into United’s midfielders out of the game. That removed the short route through the middle as an option and left the long ball. But the long ball triggered nothing for United: the second balls went to Como practically every time.

That isn’t chance, it’s running work. The sixes consistently tracked the negative pressing paths back, anticipated the long ball early, and were in front of the line before it arrived. Luis Milla covered the most ground of any player on the pitch in this pattern — recovery runs after being played past, doubling up on the ball, attacking the second ball. When the long ball came from Maguire or one of the full-backs, a six was regularly in front of the line with an intense sprint, sealing the centre, taking away the diagonal follow-up passes and attacking the loose balls.

Defending the long ball and extra support on the second ball.

Once the ball was on one side, that side was isolated. The near-side forward had access to the centre-back and dropped back a touch when the pass went to the full-back, so that he covered centre-back and six simultaneously and closed the square pass. The far-side forward marked the goalkeeper. That served two functions at once: it took away United’s escape route via the keeper along with the switch, and it kept a player in position for the moment of the turnover.

The double pivot worked in a deliberate half-position. The near-side six stayed tight to his man, the far-side one dropped slightly behind him — securing the space and at the same time establishing access to the far-side centre-back in United’s back three.

The last line defended largely man-for-man in the high press, and very tightly. But the decisive detail is the one that carries the whole concept: the man-marking near the ball applied only to the action in progress. Around it, players were already releasing from their assignments and orienting towards the second ball. That is only retrievable at very high intensity — and it’s the reason United’s forced long balls produced not a single usable moment.

When Como dropped a line, the man-orientation at the second height stayed exceptionally high. Here too the back-pass served as the trigger to push up as a unit and tip back into man-marking. If United were in a closed body position, the Comaschi had already pushed up far enough to attack the back-pass at the moment it was played — a pattern that led directly to the 3-0. The half-position was at work there again: closing down level with the centre-back while simultaneously prepared to push through onto the next player deeper.

In the first half Como still had problems assigning the half-positions against ManU’s 3-2-5. In this example Fernandes moves to the wing, Como fail to establish responsibility and access and are played through at mid-height.

Up to half-time, though, Como had a structural problem with this. The pure man-marking didn’t come off cleanly, because of the two sixes in front of the line, one should really have tucked in and taken over the half-position for the centre-backs. That didn’t happen consistently. The consequence: a centre-back stepped out, the space behind him stayed unoccupied, and that was exactly what United kept attacking.

The pattern of pressing onto Manchester United’s left side continued in the second half, now with Dalot at full-back. The press was still triggered by Douvikas, with the ball-near winger pushing up as a second striker to press the centre-back. Paz focused on the visitors’ ball-near six and Couto pushed through onto Dalot. Through the central connections the game was isolated on one side.

At half-time Como adjusted. One six moved directly into the line, with the far-side winger pushing in onto the six — they now pressed out of a 5-2-1-2. Lucas Da Cunha pressed through from the right onto Maguire, while Nico Paz picked up United’s six. The central forward pressed the central centre-back and from there carried on to the goalkeeper. The far-side wide midfielder and the remaining six were poised — one onto United’s free six, one onto the far-side centre-back — again in that half-position between opponent and the space to be protected.

ManU building up high against the man-oriented Italians.

The effect was immediately visible. Como controlled the half-positions, got access right into the last line throughout, and set the rhythm of the match from then on. The adjustment solved exactly the problem the first half had left open, without changing the underlying principle.

In the final phase Como defended from a very deep line inside the box. That left barely any space behind the line to play into — which United hardly looked for anyway. The visitors attacked little depth and rarely occupied the width in the final third, so Como could stay compact without being punished for it.

Accordingly, a lot of players stood very centrally in and in front of the box. That produced numerous cover shadows and a numerical superiority around the ball, which allowed them to steer actively within the block rather than merely react. At times they deliberately steered inside, to guide the opponent into the prepared congestion and cover from a deeper position.

Deep back line in the box, centre closed and play steered inside. The box defending allowed the English side shots from distance.

The crucial point is that Como left the gaps open not despite this set-up but because of it. If you pack the box and secure the depth, you give up the space in front of the line — and that is exactly where United were supposed to shoot from.

The numbers vindicate the decision. Taking the conceded goal out of the box, Como allowed nine shots from outside the penalty area across ninety minutes, worth roughly 0.24 xG combined; only three of them were even on target. On top of that, of the four shots inside the box, two came from at least 14 to 15 metres and effectively belong in the long-range category as well. United accepted the invitation, and the shooting quality was perfectly decent — it’s just that with that scoring probability over a whole match, the most likely outcome is precisely what happened.

1-0, Nico Paz (2′) — The goal comes before United are even set. Como take the goal kick quickly and play straight to the full-back, luring the visitors into the press — only not cleanly. Mason Mount triggers the pressure and is played past, and on the second ball he goes into the duel as well instead of holding the middle and looking for the space where he could pick the ball up. That is exactly what opens the centre, the space for the second ball is there, and Paz can play it through.

What follows is the transition principle in its purest form: a quick switch to the diagonal side, then short passes against a very deep line. Matheus Cunha doesn’t pick up the player behind him and leaves the ten space open, where Paz is standing. He needs two touches because neither Cunha from above nor Maguire from his distance gets into the duel. Lammens, for his part, doesn’t defend the goal with his cover shadow but takes the near post — which leaves the far corner to a strong left foot. The curled finish into it is quality, though.

2-0, Anastasios Douvikas (49′, assist Paz) — Same zone, same cause. Bryan Mbeumo doesn’t choose to shut the centre but closes the centre-back, which creates the space for Da Cunha, who drives forward and plays Martin Baturina in. Centrally, Mount again fails to pick up his man. Out wide, Amad Diallo, introduced at half-time, generates no pressure centrally, so Mazraoui can’t take over the deep wide player in the first place.

The result is Paz behind the opponent, in the ten space, dribbling unopposed at goal. Matthijs de Ligt establishes no reference to Douvikas’s run in behind and slips as well — a one-against-one with the goalkeeper that Douvikas finishes coolly.

3-0, Nico Paz (57′) — The only goal that comes directly out of the work against the ball. Como are in the midfield press in a closed position with access to the inside lane, win the ball there and punish a lapse of concentration in the visitors’ build-up. At the end, Paz is alone in front of Lammens again.

4-0, Jacobo Ramón (76′, assist Kaiki) — This goal comes from a longer possession spell and shows the assignment problem in its clearest form. Mazraoui is too narrow and has no access to the wide player. Diallo stands in the space with access to neither the central player nor the full-back nor the winger — an orientation that doesn’t work in any direction.

Como switch to Máximo Perrone, who can slide the ball out wide because the space is simply free. From there comes the cross from an open body position, and in the centre Como run a two-against-one at the end of which Ramón scores.

4-1, Joshua Zirkzee (90′) — Como’s only lapse, and one in a phase in which the team had already dropped very deep. United get the ball to the wing, it’s two against two in the box, and the cross is initially only feinted. Zirkzee orients himself well forwards at that moment, while Como’s defenders are already stepping out after the feinted cross. Diallo’s ball in behind the line is strong, the offside call is razor-thin in his favour, and Zirkzee takes two touches before finishing one-against-one with the goalkeeper. A blemish, not a structural problem.

The common denominator — Three of Como’s four goals come in the same zone: the space in front of and behind United’s line that nobody takes responsibility for. Mount is involved in two of those scenes through an assignment he never establishes, Diallo in two more after coming on. Nico Paz is at the heart of three of the four goals, twice as scorer, once as the provider — always out of the opponent’s blind side.

Como’s 4-1 has one common cause across every phase, and it lies less in any single idea than in its execution under continuous load. Out of possession they steered, isolated and speculated on the second ball — with man-marking that applied near the ball only for the action in progress and was already dissolved around it. In possession they tied opponents in, switched play and worked systematically into the space behind. Both require eleven players to anticipate a teammate’s decision, and both functioned over the full distance.

The connecting detail is the half-position. In the press, the far-side six stood between opponent and the space to be protected; in possession, the eights worked out of the same zone, and the full-backs occupied it flat in order to vacate the wing for exactly those players. Como, in other words, occupied the position in both directions from which two jobs can be done at once — which is why the side could neither be played past in the press nor be pinned in the build-up.

For United the diagnosis is more uncomfortable than the result. The problem wasn’t individual errors but that the team never, in any phase, got into the duels it needed. The mixture of half man-orientation and a plus-one in the last line funded no access up front, and without access every decision is made too late — from Mount in the centre to Mbeumo failing to secure the far-side depth. Carrick’s double change at half-time was meant to bring physicality to the six with Carlos Baleba, but it changed nothing about the assignments, and in Diallo a player came on wide who reached neither one opponent nor the other in his space. That the energy ran out in the end was a consequence rather than a cause.

That leaves the context. A 4-1 against Manchester United is no proof in itself, and Como’s finishing was exceptionally efficient on the night. What showed regardless of the result, though, is a team with a recognisable, rehearsed answer in all four phases of the game — and one that put a technical maturity on the pitch in possession that you wouldn’t readily credit to a side promoted two years ago.

#2661. Lionel Messi joins Gianluigi Buffon among the oldest top-ten Ballon d’Or finishers in history

Lionel Messi has added another milestone to his career by finishing fifth in the 2026 Ballon d’Or at 39 years and four months old. The Inter Miami captain did not attend the gala in London, but his place among the best players of the year has put him in the history books of the award.

After finishing in fifth position, the Argentine became the third-oldest player ever to finish inside the top ten of the Ballon d’Or. Only Gianluigi Buffon and Stanley Matthews, the winner of the first edition, were older when they reached that mark.

Buffon was 39 years and 10 months old when he reached the top ten in 2017, just a few months older than Messi now, while Matthews was 41 years old when he won the inaugural award in 1956.

Harry Kane won the award, with Kylian Mbappe second, Lamine Yamal third and Rodri fourth, one place ahead of Messi. The Argentine’s fifth place came after a standout World Cup in which he scored eight goals and added four assists, won the Silver Ball and led Argentina to the final. With eight Ballon d’Or trophies already in his collection, he remains the most decorated player in the history of the award.

For comparison, the last time Cristiano Ronaldo finished among the top ten players in the Ballon d’Or was in 2021, when he placed sixth while playing for Manchester United. The Portuguese forward was 36 years and nine months old when the ceremony took place in November of that year, more than two and a half years younger than Messi is now.

Lionel Messi and Harry Kane stand alone as only two players and Ballon d’Or winners to achieve incredible goalscoring record in 21st century

That remains the last time the five-time winner cracked the top ten. He has remained outside it in every edition since, while Messi, who won the award in 2021 and again in 2023, has returned to the top five at an age when Ronaldo was no longer in the conversation for the award.

Messi’s fifth-place finish in 2026 is his 17th appearance in the Ballon d’Or voting, a career that includes eight wins, the most in the history of the award. These are all of his results:

Legend Gianluigi Buffon joined the exodus in Italy FA after missing out the qualification to the 2026 World Cup, while Pep Guardiola has been reportedly linked to be the next head coach of the Azzurri.

Italian legend Gianluigi Buffon surprised the soccer world by leaving Lionel Messi and Cristiano Ronaldo out of his pick for the greatest player he ever faced.

Gianluigi Buffon, one of soccer's most iconic goalkeepers, has sparked significant debate with his declaration that Neymar is the best player he has ever played with.

#2660. FC Koln vs Union Berlin - prediction, team news, lineups

By Joshua Cole | 25 Oct 2026 20:13

FC Koln welcome Union Berlin to the RheinEnergieSTADION on Tuesday evening for a DFB-Pokal second-round tie, with both sides looking to secure a place in the last 16.

Tuesday's knockout encounter is the first of two meetings between these sides in the space of five days, with the teams due to meet again in the Bundesliga in Berlin next Sunday, and Rene Wagner's side will be looking to make home advantage count after being held to a goalless draw by Schalke on Saturday, while the visitors arrive following a 1-1 draw away to Augsburg.

Koln will be frustrated by their inability to turn possession and attacking pressure into goals against Schalke, with Isak Johannesson having an effort ruled out for offside, while Said El Mala was unable to convert another opportunity during a first half in which the hosts struggled to find the necessary precision.

The result means the hosts have won just one of their last six league matches, losing three and drawing two, and are now winless in their last two outings (D1, L1), leaving Wagner needing a more decisive display in a competition where one poor spell can bring a team's campaign to an end.

The Billy Goats will have the advantage of playing at home, where their supporters can help generate the intensity needed to unsettle Union's defence, and they are unbeaten at the RheinEnergieSTADION this season, having recorded two wins and two draws.

Koln reached this stage by beating lower-division side Wurzburger Kickers 2-1 in the previous round, coming from behind after conceding in the first half before Johannesson's brace after the break turned the tie around.

They reached the quarter-finals of this competition in 2024-25, their best run since the 2009-10 campaign, when they also reached the last eight, and Wagner's side will now hope to make another push towards the latter stages.

Union will be determined to spoil those ambitions as they look to match their last-16 appearance from last season, with recent results suggesting that they are becoming increasingly difficult to beat.

Mauro Lustrinelli’s side held Borussia Dortmund to a goalless draw before sharing the points with Augsburg, despite Felix Uduokhai's red card forcing them to reorganise early, and they remained competitive before responding to Han-Noah Massengo's opener.

Janik Haberer scored Union's equaliser in the 74th minute, rewarding a determined recovery after the dismissal and securing back-to-back draws following a 1-0 victory over Elversberg, leaving them unbeaten in three consecutive matches (W1, D2).

The last meeting between these clubs ended 2-2 at the Stadion An der Alten Forsterei in May, and Union are unbeaten in their last two encounters with Koln, having won 1-0 at this ground in the meeting before that draw.

The Iron Ones have also enjoyed recent trips to this stadium, winning two of their last three visits, while they have lost just one of their last seven matches against Koln, recording four wins and two draws in that sequence.

Koln remain without Timo Hubers, Thijs Dallinga, Ellyes Skhiri and Rav van den Berg, who are all recovering from injuries.

Dallinga faces a lengthy absence after suffering a cruciate ligament injury, while Hubers has been recovering from a knee problem and Skhiri is dealing with an ankle injury that is expected to keep him out for an extended period.

Ragnar Ache has resumed partial training following a muscle injury and could be considered if he makes sufficient progress before kick-off, while Alessio Castro-Montes has also been managing a muscle problem.

Union have Stanley Nsoki, Josip Juranovic and Andrej Ilic unavailable through injury, although their conditions are expected to be reassessed before the cup tie.

Uduokhai was sent off against Augsburg and is set to miss Tuesday's cup match as a consequence of that dismissal.

Schwabe; Sebulonsen, Simpson-Pusey, Lochoshvili, Sosa; Kraub, Johannesson; Maina, Moore, El Mala; Bulter

Ronnow; Haberer, van den Bosch, Querfeld, Rothe; Aebischer, Khedira, Schafer; Jeong, Skarke, Burcu

Koln have home advantage, but Union's recent form and strong record at this ground suggest that the visitors can edge a closely contested tie and advance to the next round.

The hosts may create enough opportunities to threaten, but Union's recent defensive performances indicate that they can keep Koln at bay and find a way to secure victory.

Aston Villa travel to the Vitality Stadium on Wednesday night to face Bournemouth in the fourth round of the EFL Cup.

Unai Emery's side head into the tie on the back of Saturday's 2-1 victory over Manchester City, which secured their first home league win of the 2026-27 season and lifted them to ninth in the Premier League table.

Villa are currently on a four-match winning streak across all competitions, having beaten Fenerbahce and Viking in the Champions League, either side of a league victory over Newcastle United, while they booked their place in the fourth round of the EFL Cup with a 3-1 win over Coventry City on September 16.

Last time out, Johan Manzambi returned from a brief spell on the sidelines, although several other players remain unavailable, and here, Sports Mole rounds up Villa's latest injury and suspension news ahead of Wednesday's trip to Bournemouth, who secured a 3-0 victory at Manchester United on Sunday.

Possible return date: November 16 (vs. Sunderland)

Possible return date: October 28 (vs. Bournemouth)

Aston Villa have no players suspended for this match.

#2652. Lazio, Brocchi verso il Sassuolo: il vantaggio senza coppe e il rischio transizioni

L’ex centrocampista analizza le scelte di Gattuso dopo il Parma e invita a prestare attenzione alle ripartenze neroverdi.

La Lazio affronta il Sassuolo con un vantaggio nella preparazione: non avendo impegni europei, Gattuso può lavorare per un’intera settimana sulla partita e dare ai giocatori il tempo di recuperare. Cristian Brocchi individua in questi due aspetti una risorsa importante per i biancocelesti, soprattutto mentre la squadra deve fare i conti con alcune defezioni. Secondo quanto riportato da Sitiscommesse, Al Mapei Stadium, però, servirà attenzione alle transizioni rapide dei neroverdi.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

La nona giornata di Serie A 2026/27 si disputa da martedì 27 a giovedì 29 ottobre. La Lazio è rimasta da sola al comando della classifica fino al 2-1 con cui l’Inter ha battuto la Fiorentina. Nell’analisi pubblicata da Sitiscommesse, Brocchi esamina la sfida dei biancocelesti e gli altri incontri del turno.

Per Brocchi, l’assenza delle coppe consente a Gattuso di curare i dettagli dell’avversario e ridurre il turnover. Chi è chiamato a giocare più partite può inoltre recuperare meglio, presentandosi alla gara successiva in condizioni fisiche e atletiche più favorevoli.

Contro il Sassuolo, la Lazio può provare a tenere il possesso e aggredire oppure lasciare l’iniziativa ai padroni di casa. Brocchi ritiene che, in questa fase, sappia interpretare entrambe le situazioni. Il confronto con il Parma ha mostrato la sua capacità di controllare il pallone e una preparazione tattica precisa: a sinistra i biancocelesti creavano superiorità numerica all’esterno, mentre a destra isolavano Isaksen. Con il sostegno di Floriani, il danese poteva affrontare situazioni di uno contro uno e di due contro due.

Brocchi pensa che la Lazio cercherà di riproporre quella partita. La differenza, a suo giudizio, sta nella qualità del Sassuolo quando riconquista il pallone: i neroverdi possono essere più efficaci del Parma nelle ripartenze. I biancocelesti dovranno quindi evitare di lasciare spazi a una squadra capace di sfruttarli.

Il Sassuolo arriva dal 2-0 subito a Como, terza sconfitta nelle ultime quattro gare. Brocchi invita a considerare anche il valore dell’avversario: reputa il Como superiore ai neroverdi e a molte altre squadre. Per questo non attribuisce la sconfitta soltanto a possibili difficoltà di mentalità o di energie. Allo stesso tempo, ritiene difficile che il Sassuolo perda ancora; la Lazio è in salute, ma potrebbe risentire delle tante partite e del ricambio limitato.

Nell’analisi trova spazio anche l’abbraccio tra Brocchi e Gattuso, avvenuto sabato sera prima di un’intervista. Brocchi racconta che il tecnico lo ha definito un fratello e riconosce in quelle parole il legame costruito nei momenti più belli delle loro carriere, vissuti con sincerità e lealtà. I due non si vedevano da tempo per gli impegni di lavoro e per i periodi trascorsi da Gattuso all’estero.

Per Brocchi, quell’incontro ha espresso un’amicizia destinata a durare nonostante la distanza e le diverse città. Collega anche i successi del loro Milan ai rapporti nati nello spogliatoio. Ritiene che Gattuso stia cercando di creare la stessa armonia con i suoi giocatori: un’intesa che, nella sua lettura, aiuta ad affrontare le partite difficili e a condividere pienamente le vittorie.

Dopo il 3-3 dello scontro diretto, Roma e Napoli affrontano rispettivamente Cagliari e Monza. L’Inter, reduce dalla rimonta sulla Fiorentina, gioca invece sul campo del Venezia. Brocchi considera la Roma l’antagonista dell’Inter nella corsa allo scudetto, nonostante i pareggi ottenuti nei tre confronti con Inter, Como e Napoli.

Gasperini, dopo la gara di Napoli, ha parlato della sensazione di poter competere ad alti livelli in quei big match. Brocchi condivide il giudizio: a suo avviso, alla Roma manca pochissimo perché ha già compiuto un passo importante. La squadra mantiene la propria identità anche quando cambiano gli interpreti, sa vincere e riesce a reagire alle situazioni di svantaggio.

Tra le risorse giallorosse indica Gollini. Il portiere, classe 1995, ha sostituito Svilar senza farlo rimpiangere. Brocchi lo considera affidabile ed esperto, oltre che sostenuto dalla fiducia dell’allenatore. Pur avendo accettato un ruolo diverso da quello di titolare, offre secondo lui garanzie al tecnico e ai compagni.

La sfida con il Cagliari e le successive gare di campionato appaiono sulla carta alla portata della Roma. In Champions League, invece, sono previsti gli incontri con United, PSG e Sporting. Brocchi giudica la rosa abbastanza completa, pur ritenendo che manchi ancora qualcosa: richiama la vittoria sul Genoa, arrivata anche dopo sei o sette cambi. La competizione europea potrebbe incidere sul piano emotivo, ma per lui la Roma si sta già abituando a gestire questo passaggio.

Il Cagliari arriva dal 2-0 subito a Bologna. Nella valutazione di Brocchi, la sua dimensione è quella di una squadra chiamata a lottare per restare in Serie A, obiettivo raggiunto abbastanza bene nella stagione precedente. La partenza forte aveva alzato le aspettative; ora, secondo l’ex centrocampista, servono umiltà e una partita alla volta, senza lasciare che le sconfitte aprano una fase negativa.

Quanto al Napoli, Brocchi parte dai numeri difensivi: sette gol subiti in Champions League e dieci in Serie A. Dopo il 3-3 con la Roma, Allegri ha scherzato sulla possibilità di schierare anche quattro attaccanti. Secondo Brocchi, le caratteristiche della rosa e la volontà di recuperare terreno in campionato stanno portando la squadra a essere più offensiva di quella solida che il tecnico ha sempre cercato di costruire. Questa scelta può comportare qualche rete incassata in più.

Il Napoli, però, ha a suo giudizio giocatori e organico per vincere ogni partita. Per questo Brocchi ritiene le prestazioni inferiori alle possibilità della squadra e pensa che avrebbe dovuto raccogliere qualche punto in più per restare vicina a Inter e Roma. Interpreta inoltre le frasi dirette di Allegri sulla società giallorossa, pronunciate a fine gara, come una reazione al nervosismo per una vittoria sfumata con il pareggio arrivato all’ultimo. Aggiunge che, nella stagione precedente, non fu la Roma a sottrarre la Champions al Milan: furono i rossoneri a perderla.

Il Monza, prossimo avversario del Napoli, ha sette punti. Varela è in testa alla classifica dei marcatori con sette gol, insieme a Raimondo del Frosinone, mentre circolano voci su un possibile acquisto da parte del Napoli a gennaio. Brocchi vede nel suo percorso l’esempio di un giovane talento che ha trovato spazio grazie a un allenatore disposto a puntare su di lui senza guardare l’età. Lo considera un possibile acquisto giusto, ma ritiene preferibile, soprattutto per chi ha poca esperienza, concludere la stagione nella squadra in cui sta giocando.

Il Monza è reduce dal 3-2 subito a Torino. Brocchi lo descrive come un gruppo giovane, coriaceo ed entusiasta: lo spirito trasmesso da Juric, nella sua lettura, rende le partite dei brianzoli aperte fino alla fine.

Sull’Inter, Brocchi respinge l’idea che contro la Fiorentina ci sia stato un problema di approccio. Ritiene che i nerazzurri stessero già dominando prima di andare sotto e richiama il numero dei tiri in porta. Dopo il vantaggio viola, la superiorità fisica e mentale ha permesso loro di ribaltare la gara. La squadra ha qualche difetto, osserva, ma in quell’incontro si è dimostrata più forte.

Pio Esposito ha iniziato la partita con la Fiorentina ed è uscito dopo circa un’ora. Brocchi non ritiene giusto criticarlo per la sostituzione: sottolinea quanto sta facendo, ricorda che è titolare della nazionale italiana e lo considera un titolare anche dell’Inter, pur dovendo competere con Thuram e Lautaro. Giudica quasi impossibile la cessione di uno dei due attaccanti.

Dopo il rigore non concesso per il contatto su Thuram, Chivu ha invitato a reagire agli errori arbitrali anziché lamentarsene, in linea con un messaggio espresso da Gattuso sabato sera. Brocchi condivide questa posizione. A suo giudizio, quest’anno il gioco è più continuo, ci sono meno interruzioni e perdite di tempo e gli allenatori possono accettare più facilmente qualche errore tecnico se le partite restano piacevoli.

Il Venezia ha perso 1-0 con il Genoa. Brocchi giudica complicata la sua situazione anche sul piano mentale: la paura di non risalire la classifica si accompagna alla prospettiva di dover cercare tre punti contro quella che considera la squadra più forte del campionato. Ricorda però che il Venezia ha già battuto il Napoli e può trovare uno stimolo nella possibilità di sorprendere la prima della classe.

#2651. Ballon d'Or 2026 winner and rankings predicted by AI as brutal Michael Olise verdict delivered

By Ben Knapton | 25 Oct 2026 21:00, Last updated: 25 Oct 2026 21:00

The 2026 Ballon d'Or ceremony is drawing ever closer, as in just under 48 hours' time, the prestigious prizes will be handed out to the globe's finest in London.

The Ballon d'Or returns to England's capital to celebrate the 70th anniversary of the inaugural edition - won by Sir Stanley Matthews - and it is only fitting that another Three Lions legend in Harry Kane is the favourite to take home the accolade this time around.

However, England's all-time top scorer faces the stiffest of stiff competition from World Cup winner Lamine Yamal, Champions League Player of the Season Khvicha Kvaratskhelia, a still Ballon d'Or-less Kylian Mbappe and, of course, nine-time chasing Lionel Messi.

Ahead of Monday's big reveal, Sports Mole asked AI to predict the full ranking of the 2026 men's Ballon d'Or nominees - see the results below!

The firm bookies' favourite to become England's first Ballon d'Or winner since Michael Owen a quarter of a century ago, Kane has also been predicted to hoist the Golden Ball aloft by AI.

Despite not leading Bayern Munich to Champions League glory or inspiring England to World Cup stardom, Kane racked up an astronomical 73 goals for club and country in the 2025-26 season, 61 for Bayern and 12 for the Three Lions.

Kane comfortably topped the Bundesliga scoring charts with 36 strikes en route to winning the German top flight for the second year running, while also firing in 14 Champions League goals, second only to Mbappe's 15.

However, the latter has to settle for third place, despite also taking home the La Liga and World Cup Golden Boot, becoming the highest scorer of all time in the men's mundial with 22 strikes to his name.

Sandwiched in between Mbappe and Kane in the AI charts is Barcelona and Spain phenom Yamal, whose lack of individual impact at the World Cup has perhaps fatally harmed his chances of pipping the England skipper to the top prize.

World Cup Golden Ball winner Rodri and eight-time winner Messi complete the top five, the latter pushed high up the rankings thanks to his sensational World Cup campaign and everlasting sentimental attachment to the Ballon d'Or.

Last year's winner Ousmane Dembele is down in eighth spot after struggling with a couple of injuries in 2025-26, while Arsenal's Declan Rice is the only current Premier League player to make the top 10 in the AI prediction.

The England midfielder interestingly ranks higher than Michael Olise, despite the ex-Crystal Palace winger producing a ridiculous 53 goal involvements in 52 Bayern matches in 2025-26, as well as seven World Cup assists.

Olise just ranks higher than 13th-placed Jude Bellingham, who along with William Saliba, Marc Cucurella and Achraf Hakimi are all above Erling Haaland in the projection.

UCL Player of the Season Kvaratskhelia - recently ruled out for the season with an ACL injury - is only 19th owing to Georgia's World Cup absence, joining a cluster of Paris Saint-Germain players in the bottom half alongside Joao Neves, Vitinha, Marquinhos and Nuno Mendes.

Meanwhile, Mexican World Cup star Julian Quinones props up the table in 30th place, just below Sadio Mane and Bruno Fernandes, whose record Premier League assist numbers fail to make up for Manchester United's trophyless campaign.

#2650. Alkmaar moves protest against Israeli fans amid safety concerns

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

The mayor of Alkmaar, Anja Schouten, has ordered two demonstrations against Thursday’s Europa League match between AZ and Hapoel Be’er Sheva to be moved away from the stadium because of concerns about potential disorder.

Hundreds of people are expected to attend the protests against the participation of Israeli clubs in European competition, which have been organised by pro-Palestinian groups Boycott 4 Palestine and Week 4 Palestine.

The protests were originally due to take place outside the main entrance to the AFAS Stadium, but Schouten ordered the demonstrators to gather near the Kooimeer roundabout, the main road junction leading to the stadium, instead.

“It is not possible to take adequate management measures to prevent disorder and separate fans going to the stadium from demonstrators,” Schouten said.

“This [new] location allows us to facilitate the safe arrival and departure of demonstrators. We also need to prevent confrontations taking place with other groups.”

A post shared by Week.4PalestineNL (@week.4palestinenl)

In a briefing note to councillors, the mayor said there were indications that supporters of the Israeli club were planning to travel to Alkmaar in spite of the ban.

“There are fears that protesters will come into conflict with each other, with supporters’ groups or with the police, that vandalism could ensue, or that the safety of people attending the match could be endangered,” she wrote.

Schouten came under fire from opposition parties on the city council last week for her decision to ban Israeli supporters from travelling to the match. AZ Alkmaar cancelled ticket sales to visiting fans in the wake of the mayor’s decision.

She said the decision was based on public order concerns rather than “political considerations”, following the riots surrounding a Europa League game between Ajax and Maccabi Tel Aviv in Amsterdam two years ago.

Schouten said it was “virtually impossible” to keep groups apart outside the stadium, especially as many visiting fans were expected to stay in Amsterdam for the game.

Alkmaar had originally said Israeli supporters were welcome at the game, but the mayor changed her mind after her counterpart in Amsterdam, Femke Halsema, made an “urgent” appeal not to allow a repeat of the scenes in the capital in 2024.

VVD councillor John van der Rhee accused Schouten of succumbing to a “powerplay” by the mayor of Amsterdam.

#2649. Meerdink double seals Netherlands victory in Serbia

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 10 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 9 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 8 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 6 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 5 of the Eredivisie ...

Is AZ Alkmaar's academy now the best in the Netherlands? ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 4 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 3 of the Eredivisie ...

It's time for our BIG Dutch Eredivisie season preview! Football-Oranje's ...

A new season of Dutch football is here! Football-Oranje's Michael ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 10 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 9 of the Eredivisie ...

Xavi Hernandez has his first win as Netherlands head coach as a Mexx Meerdink double led Oranje to a 2-1 win over Serbia in the Nations League.

Xavi made two changes from the side that drew with Germany as Mexx Meerdink replaced the injured Brian Brobbey up front while Joey Veerman came in for Quinten Timber.

Netherlands dominated from the start but they were forced into an early change when Cody Gakpo suffered a knock and Ruben van Bommel came on.

After half an hour, Crysencio Summerville was clipped in the box and Meerdink made it 1-0 from the penalty spot. It was almost 2-0 before the break but Denzel Dumfries could not convert when through on goal.

Serbia made a double change at the break with Luka Jovic and Dusan Tadic coming on. A minute into the second half, the change worked as Jovic was given space in the box to net after Netherlands failed to clear the ball.

Netherlands went in search of the lead again but Tijjani Reijnders blazed a huge chance over while Ruben van Bommel hit the post. Meerdink did eventually make it 2-1 with a lovely finish after a quick 1-2 with substitute Guus Til.

There was to be no hattrick for Meerdink as he was brought off and Netherlands played the final ten minutes with Til through the middle.

Serbia pressed for a late equaliser but the Netherlands had a golden chance to make it 3-1 when Til found himself in on goal. He had Summerville and Van Bommel to his left but overhit the pass and the later’s finish was too weak to find the net.

The Netherlands held on for the victory which takes them to the top of the group with a clash against Greece next on Thursday.

#2644. VfL Bochum vs Mainz 05 - prediction, team news, lineups

By Lewis Nolan | 25 Oct 2026 21:39

Mainz 05 will hope to bounce back from defeat when they travel to face VfL Bochum at Ruhrstadion on Tuesday in the DFB-Pokal's second round.

The visitors suffered a 3-2 loss against Werder Bremen in the Bundesliga on Saturday, whereas the hosts come into their next clash having drawn 1-1 with Wolfsburg in the second tier on October 23.

Bochum's season in the 2. Bundesliga has been disappointing, with the club in 14th place with just eight points after nine matchweeks.

Their stalemate with Wolfsburg saw them score just their sixth goal in the second tier, and it was the first time they found the back of the net at all in any of their last three games.

Manager Uwe Rosler has failed to guide his team to a victory in their five most recent fixtures, and a loss on Tuesday would be his side's fourth in six matches.

The hosts are not only winless in their past six meetings with Mainz, but they have also lost each of their last three encounters with the visitors, including when they were beaten 4-1 in May 2025.

A major concern for Rosler will be that Bochum have failed to win any of their five outings at home in 2026-27, suffering three losses.

Mainz's campaign has been positive, with the club in seventh place with 10 points after seven matchweeks, though they did suffer their second defeat in three games against Bremen.

Die Nullfunfer have been porous at the back having conceded 13 times in the Bundesliga, with all of those goals conceded in their last five top-flight contests.

The visitors only claimed nine points from their first 16 league fixtures in 2025-26, so despite their defensive struggles, they have still made significant progress.

Head coach Urs Fischer has experienced mixed success recently, as while his team did lose against Bremen, a victory on Tuesday would be their third in five games.

Mainz's record away from has been perfect this season given they have triumphed in all four clashes on the road, scoring 19 goals, though half of those strikes came in their 9-0 victory against Krieschow in the DFB-Pokal's first round on August 23.

If Bochum use the same 4-2-3-1 system that they deployed earlier in the DFB-Pokal, perhaps fans will see centre-backs Michael Steinwender and Karol Mets stationed behind Mats Pannewig and Tom Meyer.

Striker Philipp Hofmann could be supported by attacking midfielder Berkan Taz, as well as wingers Koji Miyoshi and Christian Rasmussen.

As for Mainz, they could field a similar lineup to the one that started in the first round of the DFB-Pokal, though with defender Dominik Kohr out until January, Danny da Costa, Stefan Posch and Kacper Potulski could feature in defence.

It would be surprising to see regulars like Kaishu Sano or Jae-Sung Lee in midfield, with Eric Martel and Paul Nebel likely inclusions instead.

In the frontline, Benedict Hollerbach and Ransford Konigsdorffer could start in order to give Sheraldo Becker and Phillip Tietz a break from the intense demands of first-team football.

Horn; Klaiber, Steinwender, Mets, Wittek; Meyer, Pannewig; Miyoshi, Taz, Rasmussen; Hofmann

Zentner; Da Costa, Posch, Potulski; Widmer, Nebel, Martel, Amiri, Mwene; Konigsdorffer, Hollerbach

Despite the inconsistencies of Mainz in the Bundesliga, anything other than a comfortable away win would be surprising.

Bochum could threaten the visitors' backline, especially as their opponents have been vulnerable of late, but they should not be expected to get the better of a top-flight side.

(ANSA) - LISBONA, 27 OTT - Cristiano Ronaldo figura tra i giocatori pre-convocati dalla nazionale portoghese per le prossime partite di Nations League, aprendo la strada al suo rientro in squadra dopo lo scontro con l'allenatore Jorge Jesus. Essendo la sanzione retroattiva, il periodo di sospensione è decorso dal giorno in cui il giocatore ha lasciato il ritiro, il che non gli impedisce di tornare a disposizione il mese prossimo. #2638. Pellegrini links former Man Utd flop Antony’s goalless run to ‘excessive anxiety’

By Alexis Pereira | 25 Oct 2026 22:02

Real Betis boss Manuel Pellegrini has attributed Antony’s goalless start to the 2026-27 season to what he described as ‘excessive anxiety’, while insisting that the Brazilian winger’s goals will arrive as he continues to influence matches elsewhere.

Pellegrini spoke on Saturday ahead of Sunday’s La Liga fixture at Celta Vigo, offering his most detailed assessment of the 25-year-old’s form since the season began.

‘I think Antony is putting too much pressure on himself right now,’ the Chilean manager said. ‘He is a player who runs a great deal, wins the ball back and gets involved in many moves. Perhaps due to excessive anxiety, he is missing that final pass or that touch of quality.’

The statistics reflect a player creating chances but failing to convert them. Across 11 appearances this season, Antony has registered 44 shots — 15 on target — while missing three big chances and hitting the woodwork twice, according to Sofascore. His sole direct contribution has been one assist. The return is starkly removed from his 2025-26 campaign, when he scored 14 goals and provided 10 assists across 46 matches despite playing through a pubitis problem that required pain relief to keep him out of the treatment room for the majority of the season.

Pellegrini nonetheless stopped short of expressing any doubt. ‘He is a player who contributes a great deal to the team’s performance,’ the boss added. ‘The goals and those precise final passes will come. He is very aware of this and he is trying to correct and improve.’

The frustration Antony felt became visible on Wednesday when he was substituted after 64 minutes of the 1-0 Champions League win over Feyenoord. Unable to use his speed and dribbling on the right flank as effectively as he wanted, the former Manchester United winger threw his jacket to the ground on the touchline before heading straight to the dressing room.

Pellegrini described the substitution as a ‘tactical decision’ and noted that Antony returned to the bench shortly afterwards to be with his team-mates. The episode contrasted with the resilience the player showed throughout last season, when he refused to stop playing despite significant physical pain.

Betis have made a strong start to the season with a 100 per cent Champions League record and 19 points from nine La Liga matches. Antony’s form remains the one open question for Pellegrini as the campaign develops.

#2637. Lazio-Roma, biglietti a 10 euro per gli Aquilotti: la prelazione

La vendita riservata si apre il 27 ottobre alle 12:00 e termina il 29 ottobre alle 23:59; coinvolge anche i settori Sud.

Per Lazio-Roma, in programma domenica 13 dicembre 2026 alle 12:30, è prevista una prelazione sui biglietti dal 27 al 29 ottobre per gli abbonati Aquilotti e per quelli dei settori Sud. Secondo quanto riportato da S.S. Lazio, La vendita riservata aprirà martedì 27 ottobre alle 12:00 e terminerà giovedì 29 ottobre alle 23:59.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Gli abbonati Aquilotti della stagione 2026/27, il cui abbonamento non comprende questa partita come già comunicato durante la campagna abbonamenti, potranno acquistare un biglietto a 10 euro sul proprio posto.

Anche gli abbonati 2026/27 dei Distinti Sud Est e della Curva Maestrelli non hanno diritto alla gara con il proprio abbonamento, secondo quanto comunicato nella campagna abbonamenti. Per loro la prelazione consente l’acquisto di un posto in un settore alternativo. Tra gli abbonati dei due settori sono compresi anche gli Aquilotti.

Per utilizzare la prelazione occorre inserire online il codice come coupon oppure presentare uno dei codici riportati sulle card. Per l’abbonamento digitale con la Millenovecento il codice inizia con 032; per quello con la Eagle inizia con EAG. Sui biglietti acquistati online si applica una commissione di servizio del 3,80%.

Il tagliando Ridotto Under 16 è acquistabile dai ragazzi nati dal 1° gennaio 2010. Il Ridotto Under 14 Aquilotto è destinato ai nati dal 1° gennaio 2012. I bambini di 4 anni nati dal 1° gennaio 2022 entrano gratuitamente e senza biglietto, mostrando un documento d’identità o la tessera sanitaria.

Chi acquista fisicamente un biglietto in un punto vendita deve esibire il proprio documento d’identità: senza, il titolo di accesso non può essere emesso, ai sensi del D.L. 8/2/2010 n. 8. Non sono ritenute idonee alla vendita le patenti di ultima generazione prive dell’indicazione del luogo di residenza.

Nella vendita libera una persona potrà acquistare al massimo quattro tagliandi, secondo le disposizioni dell’Osservatorio sulle Manifestazioni Sportive. Per le altre eventuali tre persone è sufficiente presentare anche una copia del documento d’identità. All’ingresso, invece, l’identità sarà controllata per ciascuno spettatore: tutti, minorenni compresi, dovranno mostrare un documento.

#2636. No Picnic: inquiry reveals Dutch cycling team’s toxic culture

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

The Dutch cycling team Picnic-PostNL has been silently struggling with an oppressive atmosphere and culture of intimidation, according to an investigation published by broadcaster NOS and cycling platform In De Waaier.

Many of the team’s former members have been left traumatised and described a “culture of fear” and “unsafe working conditions”.

Reporters confidentially spoke with 71 current and former employees of the team, which competes in the World Tour, the sport’s top division. It currently employs around 120 people. Many people described how management would punish criticism and ignore mental health complaints.

One interviewee described the management structure as a “dictatorship” and another said team members were treated as “puppets”.

This began after the team’s successes in the Tour de France in 2013 and 2014, when German rider Marcel Kittel won stages as a sprinter. After that, the managers became paranoid about losing control of the team, one person told NOS.

More rules were established and people were told “everyone is replaceable.”

After Dutch racer Tom Dumoulin won the 2017 Giro d’Italia, one of cycling’s three Grand Tours, the managers became even more “radicalised”, according to another interviewee.

Dutch rider Martijn Tusveld described how he was dropped from major races for questioning the team’s tactics. He became so anxious he considered harming himself. He left the team and said he is still mentally unwell.

Another, who was suffering mentally, said he was told by sports director Rudi Kemna “I don’t believe in psychologists” after he asked for help.

The Dutch professional cyclists’ association VVBW told NOS it had heard similar accounts over the past 18 months, with some riders calling in tears.

NOS has asked the team owner Iwan Spekenbrink for a comment but he has so far refused to reply to any allegations in person or in writing.

An injury-hit Netherlands side battled back to draw 2-2 against Greece in the Nations League.

Xavi was forced into changes with Cody Gakpo and Jan Paul van Hecke dropping out of the squad. Tijjani Reijnders and Ryan Gravenberch also dropped to the bench with Quinten Timber, Guus Til, Jurrien Timber and Ruben van Bommel starting.

Greece have been in good form recently and their confidence was clear from the start. They had a goal disallowed for offside before Fotis Ioannidis made it 1-0 after 23 minutes.

The Netherlands offered little going forward and in stoppage time, a bad back pass from Van Bommel led to Giorgos Masouras sliding in a second.

Xavi made a triple change as Gravenberch, Noa Lang and Reijnders came on and they all made a big difference as the Netherlands dominated the second half.

Virgil van Dijk headed one back before Denzel Dumfries had two goals disallowed for offside. Xavi turned to Joshua Zirkzee and debutante Gjivai Zechiel in hope of an equaliser.

The equaliser came in the last minute as Zechiel’s overhead kick dropped into the path of Reijnders who blasted the ball in. Zechiel then came close to a winner but his header was straight at the goalkeeper.

A battling point for the Netherlands, who stay unbeaten under Xavi with a game at home to Serbia to come on Sunday.

#2630. Barcelona 1-2 Real Madrid: Highlights, stats, man of the match as Kylian Mbappe makes the difference

By Ellis Stevens | 25 Oct 2026 22:07

Real Madrid claimed a dramatic 2-1 victory against Barcelona at Spotify Camp Nou in La Liga on Sunday.

Barcelona and Real Madrid both created plenty of chances during the first 45 minutes, but it was Los Blancos who went into the break with the lead thanks to Kylian Mbappe's goal - albeit one the striker knew little about.

Raphinha continued his phenomenal scoring start to the season with a 60th-minute equaliser for Barcelona, marking the Brazilian's 13th goal in nine league games, and that appeared set to seal the 1-1 draw as the game tickets towards the full-time whistle.

However, there was one final twist in the tale as Eduardo Camavinga superbly crafted a chance for Mbappe to seal a late victory with just minutes remaining, securing Real Madrid a crucial three points in the title race.

Los Blancos' win means they now trail La Liga leaders Barcelona by just three points, with the hosts' nine-game winning run at the beginning of the league campaign coming to a devastating end.

Jose Mourinho has just recorded another masterclass, leading Real Madrid to a crucial 2-1 victory against Barcelona at Camp Nou.

Los Blancos were the considerable underdogs headed into this fixture and trailed their hosts by six points in La Liga, meaning a victory for Barcelona would severely dent Real Madrid's title hopes.

However, a stern defensive performance and moments of brilliance in attack saw Mourinho's men claim a potentially pivotal 2-1 victory over Hansi Flick's side, meaning Real Madrid have cut the gap at the top of the table to just three points.

Real Madrid superbly disrupted Barcelona's attacking players throughout the 90 minutes, with Pedri, Lamine Yamal and several others often unable to display their usual attacking quality.

At the other end, Real Madrid were decisive when it mattered, with Mbappe scoring a brace - albeit with one goal he knew very little about - to lead the visitors to a brilliant triumph.

While Mourinho will be delighted with how his side carried out his orders, Flick will be left hugely disappointed with aspects of his team's performance.

Barcelona were often sloppy and toothless in attack, giving the ball away far too easily, while they were physically bested in midfield and looked vulnerable in defence against Real Madrid's dangerous attackers.

The result means the title race has been blown wide open again at this early stage of the season, with Barcelona now leading Atletico Madrid by two points and Real Madrid by just three.

#2629. Faris Moumbagna out of Marseille’s plans

Faris Moumbagna (26) has been frozen out at Olympique de Marseille. He does not feature in the club’s plans for the remainder of the season, as per a report from L’Équipe.

His performance off the bench in the loss to Olympiacos in the UEFA Europa League earlier this month incited anger from Bruno Genesio. He was sent to the reserves in order to regain his fitness.

A return to the first-team set-up is not on the cards. He had been afforded a second chance due to the swathe of departures over the summer. It left OM particularly light in attack. He has made six appearances in all competitions, but now looks unlikely to add to that.

Genesio will continue to count on Neal Maupay as a backup to Amine Gouiri. Moumbagna, however, will now sit in reserve, having failed to seize his second chance. The Cameroon international’s contract with Marseille runs until 2028.
